# PLANNED / BACKLOG — every unbuilt-but-intended thing, in one place

> **Companion to `CURRENT-STATE.md`.** That file says what IS; this one says what is **intended and
> not yet done**. Assembled 2026-08-26 (Prompt 141) by sweeping every design brief, prompt
> "follow-ups" section, gap-audit tail, status ledger and archived STATUS block in the repo.
>
> **Nothing here is a new idea.** Every row cites **where it came from** so you can read the original
> reasoning, and so a future consolidation can prove nothing was invented or dropped. Rows are kept
> even when they look dead — *a contemplated feature is never deleted, only re-ranked or explicitly
> retired with a reason.*
>
> **Before building any row: apply the P131 lens.** Classify the gap first —
> **(a)** the answer is already on-box and STRUCTURED → deterministic plumbing, **not** an LLM;
> **(b)** on-box but UNSTRUCTURED text → an LLM fits; **(c)** not on-box at all → neither, an LLM
> would fabricate. Two of the top-ranked local-model gaps turned out to be (a) and (c) once measured.
>
> **And re-measure the row before you build it.** Several rows below carry dates from July; the
> standing doctrine is that a dated blocker is a hypothesis, not a fact.

**Legend** — 🔴 not started · 🟡 partially built · 🟢 designed/specced, ready to build ·
⏳ built, pending a manual apply/flip · 👤 needs Scott (tenant / human decision) · ⚪ optional/roadmap ·
🔍 needs re-measure before it can be ranked honestly.

| 🟡 **PRI6** | 👤 **Response reviewed 2026-09-12 — three real defects found and fixed, both Section-0 guesses in the prompt turned out wrong; held at 🟡 pending confirmation `Dialysis` PR #7409 is merged.** (a) No real time cap on a `cms_medicare_clinics`/`facility_patient_counts` run exists — `PRI5`'s 2h/6h reclaim windows both rested on the same "~90 minute" observation this run's own 17.9-hour lifespan disproved. (b) The actual root cause: `ingest_medicare_clinics()` calls `acquire_ingestion_lock(force=force or force_refresh)`, and the daily production entry point hard-codes `force_refresh=True` on every call — so `force` resolves true and unconditionally reclaims the pipeline's own just-opened row every single day (caught live: a row reclaiming itself 0.0h after creation). (c) Fixed both root causes — the self-reclaim bug, and a separate `facility_patient_counts` defect where `release_ingestion_lock(status="success")` sat in an unreachable `else:` after a `return` inside `try`, so a successful no-op run's lock never closed. **Neither of Section 0's two hypotheses (different Railway service vs. silently reused stale lock) was correct** — one continuous process self-reclaimed its own row on startup, then ran on unaffected. **Materially bigger than the prompt framed it**: a parallel session's own live re-check the same day (`B6d-cms-restart`, re-verified 2026-09-12) independently found `cms_ingestion` failing 34/36 runs over 30 days, `last_success_at` frozen since 2026-04-04, with recent failures reading exactly `"Reclaimed by ingestion_lock (force) after 0.0h"` — this is that bug, hitting the daily production schedule for months, cross-referenced both directions. Tests: 12 new, 8/12 independently confirmed red against pre-fix code (mutation-style), full suite 3,131/0 failed. **PR `sbriggssjc/Dialysis#7409` opened this round** — merge status unconfirmed, asked Scott directly (same recurring ambiguity: "This PR is merged" most likely refers to the `life-command-center` docs PR that filed this review, not the `Dialysis`-side PR). Full writeup: `docs/claude-code/responses/done/PRI6-ingestion-lock-survives-redeploy-and-reclaim-safety-window.response.md`. | 🟡 👤 | Cowork 2026-09-12, response reviewed same day |

## P0b — ⭐ BD PIPELINE FUNNEL (re-audit 2026-08-28, Scott's framing: property → owner → chain → developer)

Source: `docs/audits/BD_PIPELINE_FUNNEL_AUDIT_2026-08-28.md`. **Every prior audit measured queued
work; this one measures the funnel.** gov: 13,835 live properties → 6,362 with an LCC owner link →
**149 with 2+ historical owner links (1.1%)**. Against the goal — *connected ownership history back
to the developer across target markets* — **that 1.1% is the number that matters.**

They did not fire because at reclaim time each row was still `started`: **the process is hard-KILLED, so there is no exception to capture** and the prescribed fix would have produced nothing. Every populated `error_log` is a **janitor artifact** that overwrote the diagnostic slot. ⚠️ Also corrected: the two `started`+NULL rows are **`ingestion_lock`** rows, not pipeline runs (the documented lump-the-lock footgun), and **§2's "6 success runs" are none of them `cms_ingestion`** — that pipeline has **never** reported success in the window. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-31 (second pass): that true fact does NOT dismiss the defect, and concluding so was wrong.** `get_last_ingestion_meta()` filters on `dataset_id` + `run_status` and **NOT on `source`**, so the `source='CMS'` rows ARE this pipeline's watermark — and the live one is a **zero-duration** row (`finished_at = started_at`) with `rows_upserted` NULL and every count 0. **Split by the key the CONSUMER uses, not the one that best explains the population.** **Next is Railway deploy logs (OOM/restart), which `ingestion_tracker` structurally cannot carry.** Writeup: `docs/audits/B6d_cms_step_ERROR_CHANNEL_2026-08-31.md`. | 🔴 | re-measured 2026-08-31 |
| 🚨 **B6d-cms-divert** | **THE PIPELINE RUNS, DIVERTS THE WHOLE FEED TO REVIEW, AND NEVER WRITES THE CLINIC.** Measured live 2026-08-31 19:00–19:45: a process wrote **2,148 `pending_updates` rows at ~78/min with NO `ingestion_tracker` row at all** (`tracker_rows_since 19:00 = 0` — invisible to every surface built on that table) while **`max(medicare_clinics.source_last_seen)` stayed 2026-06-25 and `refreshed_today = 0`.** It reads CMS, detects a change per clinic, queues it, and **never writes the clinic**. **So the outage is not only that runs are killed — when a run does proceed, the feed is converted into review rows instead of being ingested.** This outranks the rest of the B6d arc; read it first. | 🔴 | measured 2026-08-31 |
| 🚨 **B6d-cms-preloop** | **THE UNGUARDED WINDOW — this is the answer to *"why does no failure row carry an error"*.** The 2026-09-01 06:00 cron fired, produced **FOUR** `cms_ingestion` rows for ONE firing, and **55 minutes later all four still read `started` / `finished_at` NULL / `error_log` NULL / `notes = {}` — NO HEARTBEAT**, 0 clinics stamped (yesterday's run reached its heartbeat in 8 min). **Structure:** `run_cms_ingestion()` creates the tracker row at **line 770**; the step loop starts **~925** and sets `_run_started_at` **at the loop**. So everything between has **no heartbeat, no per-step SIGALRM, and is not counted toward the whole-run cap** — a hang there is invisible AND unbounded, and `DEFAULT_RUN_TIMEOUT_SEC = 5400` is unenforceable for exactly the window where the process is least observable. ⚠️ **It also explains the historical `abandoned` ages** (9.3h / 10.5h / 23.8h, not ~2h): the orphan sweep runs at the START of a run, so a stranded row waits for the NEXT day's cron. ⚠️ **And it is NOT the OOM hypothesis** — a hard kill and an unguarded hang produce the same row; **the heartbeat distinguishes them** (`current_step` present ⇒ entered a step; `{}` after an hour ⇒ never reached the loop). **Fix: start the whole-run clock at the run, and give the pre-loop phase its own heartbeat.** | 🔴 | measured 2026-09-01 |
| 🚨 **B6d-cms-freshness-max** | ⚠️ **CONFIRMED LIVE 23:25 — `v_feed_freshness` NOW READS `status='ok'`, `age_days=0`, `is_stale=false` WHILE 7,445 OF 8,547 CLINICS (87.1%) ARE BEYOND THE FEED'S OWN 45-DAY SLA.** The producing pass ran 20:33:51 → 22:51:07 at 1–4 clinics/min, **died at 249 clinics (2.91%)** with no taper and no `ingestion_tracker` row, and left the monitor green on the way out — the same abrupt-stop signature as the killed runs in §2. **Before this pass the outage was at least visible as 65 days stale; it is now invisible.** A `max()` FRESHNESS CHECK READS FRESH ON 2.91% COVERAGE.** `feed_freshness_registry` watches `medicare_clinics` on `ts_column='source_last_seen'` (45d) and `compute_feed_freshness` takes **`max(ts_column)`** — so **ONE stamped row takes the feed from 65 days stale to age 0**. Measured 2026-08-31 22:27 while ingestion was mid-pass: **203 of 8,547 clinics touched (2.38%), 7,489 (87.6%) still ≥30 days stale, `max` = today.** The `feed_stale` alert will auto-resolve with seven-eighths of the fleet a month behind, and at the observed **1.8 clinics/min** a full pass is **~79 hours**. **This is B6d's own "grade the threshold" lesson one layer down** — B6d graded the BOUND and never asked whether the AGGREGATE could be satisfied by a fraction of the population. A max answers *"did anything arrive"*, never *"was the feed ingested"*. Honest instrument for a per-row feed: **coverage** (`count(*) filter (where ts >= now() - bound) / count(*)`) or **p95 age**, never the max. ⚠️ **Do not read the alert auto-resolving as the outage being fixed.** | 🔴 | measured 2026-08-31 |

| **B6e-fred-sendcount** | **`ingest_fred_to_dialysis` cannot tell a real write from an idempotent no-op.** `_upsert_batch` returns `len(rows)` and posts with `Prefer: return=minimal`, so `total_written` counts rows **SENT**, not rows changed — the documented *a send counter is not a write counter* trap — and `sys.exit(0 if total_written > 0 else 1)` therefore passes on a pure no-op re-upsert. Real but secondary (the guard is about crash-vs-no-crash) and changing exit semantics is a behaviour change; not bundled into B6e-fred. **This is why B6e-fred-verify asserts on a DB delta rather than the script's tally.** | 🟡 | B6e-fred §5 |

QUESTION: NO — a schedule solves the wrong half.** `property_metadata_backfill_queue`: **1,365 rows, ALL enqueued on ONE day (2026-05-21), and `attempts = 0` on EVERY ROW.** **The drain has never processed a single row in 3.5 months.** ⚠️ **Nothing ENQUEUES either** — no new rows since that day — **so a weekly cron would drain 662 once and then run empty forever.** That is a consumer with no producer, the mirror of Class 2. **The GAPS are real though:** of the 662 `open` rows, **0 properties are gone** and only **16 have since had `year_built` filled / 16 `land_area`** — so **~646 are still genuine gaps** (`year_built` 224, `land_area` 205, and combinations). ⚠️ **But 703 rows are `captured`/RESOLVED with 0 attempts** — **51% of the original queue self-resolved via other ingestion paths**, which is both evidence the fields do fill over time and a reason to size the assessor's marginal yield before automating it. **Correct sequence: (1) RUN IT ONCE manually against the 662 and measure real yield — it has never executed, so scheduling it now would be scheduling untested code, which is how the last three silent producers happened; (2) only if the yield justifies it, build the ENQUEUER (the missing producer half); (3) only then add a schedule.**  ✅ **Also now LISTED in `INFRASTRUCTURE.md`'s job map (B6e-fred, 2026-09-01)** — its absence from that map was part of the same blindness as `fred-ingest-daily.yml`. ✅ **STEP 1 RUN 2026-09-01 — AND THE ANSWER IS DO NOT WIRE IT.** `--from-queue 25` → **`processed 25, enriched 0, fields_updated 0, errors 0, elapsed 114.8s`**. ~4.6 s/property of real elapsed work, **zero yield and zero trace**: `attempts` still 0 and `last_attempt_at`/`last_error` still NULL on **all 1,365 rows**, queue gaps unchanged (`land_area` 409 · `year_built` 404 · `building_size` 108 · `tenant` 95), **0 `properties` rows written** by the run. 🚨 **`errors: 0` with `enriched: 0` is the silent-success shape** — scheduled, it would have reported no errors weekly forever. ⚠️ **And it cannot page past what it cannot mark (Class 12, purest form): it writes nothing on EITHER outcome, so `--from-queue 25` re-selects the SAME 25 rows every run.** Stacked on the Class 8 finding already in this row (one-shot queue, no enqueuer), that is three defect classes in one unscheduled job. **Running it once before scheduling it is the only reason any of this is known.** → **B6d-assessor-marker** / **-verdict** / **-producer**. | 🟡 | measured 2026-09-01 |
| ⭐ **B6d-assessor-marker** | **PREREQUISITE to every other assessor decision: the drain must record its attempt and its REASON on both outcomes.** The 2026-09-01 run proved the columns (`attempts`, `last_attempt_at`, `last_error`) exist and **have never been written by anything** — so *the assessor genuinely has nothing for these parcels* (a real ceiling ⇒ retire the lane) and *every call is failing* (a fixable adapter) are **indistinguishable**, and 4.6 s/property says it is reaching *something*. ⚠️ **This is P136's negative-marker lesson exactly**: a worker that leaves no trace on an empty target re-selects that target forever, so the marker is what makes the lane pageable AND what makes it gradeable. FROM anon, authenticated` was a no-op because **PUBLIC** held the grant. Here `REVOKE ALL ... FROM public` is a no-op because **anon and authenticated hold EXPLICIT grants** (`proacl` reads `anon=X/postgres`, and the bare `=X` PUBLIC entry is genuinely gone) — almost certainly from Supabase's `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` at original CREATE time, which a later `CREATE OR REPLACE` preserves. `lcc_flush_provenance_events` is a live example: its migration ships `REVOKE ALL ... FROM public; GRANT EXECUTE ... TO service_role;`, which **reads as "service_role only" and is not** — `has_function_privilege('anon', ..., 'EXECUTE')` is **true**, and it is a definer writer into the append-only `field_provenance` ledger. **Assert a privilege with `has_function_privilege()`, never by reading the REVOKE you just wrote.** ⚠️ **DO NOT blanket-revoke.** `compute_feed_freshness` keeps an explicit `anon` grant BY DESIGN (the LCC cross-DB pull reads `v_feed_freshness` as anon) and revoking it would silently blind the freshness monitor; and it is unconfirmed from the sandbox whether `OPS_SUPABASE_KEY` is the service or anon key, so revoking on the flush could stop the live drain. **Triage per function: establish the caller's role first, then narrow.** Not touched by PR8 — pre-existing, unchanged in shape by it. | 🔴 | measured 2026-09-02 during PR8 |

| **PR12b** | 🟡 **`lcc_flush_provenance_events` advances its watermark PAST an errored event.** `v_max_id` is assigned before the per-event `EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS` block, so an event that fails still moves `max_event_id`, and any caller that persists that value as its cursor skips the event **permanently** — the failure is returned in `errors[]` but the event is gone. PR12 removed the only error that fired today; the shape survives for the next one. Fix: advance the watermark only on the last SUCCESSFUL event, or return the failed ids so the caller can retry. Found in PR12 §5, not changed there. | 🟡 | PR12_PROVENANCE_QUOTE_LOSS_2026-09-02.md §5 |

| **B6d-cms-escalation** | 🔴 **The producer died repeatedly and nothing escalated — and it is structural, not an oversight.** This pipeline is in **no producer registry**: its runs land in `ingestion_tracker`, which **no health view consumes**, while B6a's `run_log` → `v_pipeline_task_health` surface is gov-side. So the **45-day freshness bound was the ONLY thing that could ever catch it**, and it took two months. **A failing producer should be louder than a stale table** — the freshness monitor measures a *symptom*; nothing watched the *cause*. This is **B6a Class 21 one layer up**: B6a fixed *"a skipped step emits nothing"*; here **the step emits, into a table with no consumer.** Cheapest honest version: alert on any row reaching `abandoned`, and on `max(started_at) − max(started_at where run_status='success')` exceeding the feed's own cadence. | **B6d-cms-lock** | 🟡 **`acquire_ingestion_lock` can reclaim the pipeline's OWN rows.** `ingest_medicare_clinics` calls it with `force=force or force_refresh`, so any forced run force-reclaims every `started` row for the dataset — **including the tracker rows the same run opened seconds earlier** — and marks them `failed`. That is the origin of the *"Reclaimed after 0.0h stale"* rows. Root shape: the lock writes its own row into `ingestion_tracker` under the **same `dataset_id`**, so lock rows and pipeline run rows are indistinguishable to its own query. B6d-cms fixed only the **message** (it hardcoded `"stale"` regardless of reason — a self-contradicting *"0.0h stale"* that cost a diagnosis cycle). The semantics are untouched: it is lock behaviour, not the outage, and deserves its own change. | 🟡 | B6d-cms §7 |

## P0s — ⏸️ SECRETS EXPOSED IN THE REPO — ROTATION DEFERRED BY DECISION (Scott, 2026-08-29)

> 👤 **DECISION, 2026-08-29: all credential rotation is deferred until a second user is added to
> LCC.** Rationale: single-user, still building, private repo — the exposure has no second party to
> reach. **This is a recorded risk acceptance with a trigger, not an oversight**, and the rows stay
> open so it cannot go quiet.
>
> 🔔 **The trigger is broader than "another user" — the PRIVATE REPO is what carries this risk.**
> Rotate BEFORE any of: a second LCC user · **making the repo public** · **sharing it with a
> contractor, vendor or Northmarq IT** · a lost/compromised laptop · LCC leaving "still building"
> for anything a client touches.
>
> ✅ **SEC4 STAYS ACTIVE.** It is a guard, not a rotation: deferring rotation is a decision about the
> keys already exposed; **SEC4 is what stops the next PA export adding more.** Without it, the
> rotation you eventually do is against a moving target.
>
> ⚠️ **When rotation happens, ORDER MATTERS: rotate → update Railway → `git rm --cached` +
> `.gitignore` → only then consider history.** `git rm --cached` alone leaves the value in history
> and in every clone. **Do not reach for `filter-branch`** — this repo nearly lost a 475 MB mailbox
> that way.

⚠️ **These are credential exposures, not hygiene. They outrank every other row in this file.**
**Rotation is what neutralises them — removing a file from tracking does NOT**, because the value
remains in git history and in any clone or fork.

Also carries `LCC_HOST=https://life-command-center-nine.vercel.app` (**the retired host that still answers and still holds a service key**, P194) plus Teams tenant/team/channel IDs. ⚠️ **This is a DIFFERENT and worse exposure than SEC1**, which records the key as *"pasted in plaintext during a curl diagnostic"* — a chat paste is transient; **a tracked file is in history, in every clone, and in every fork.** **Order: (1) ROTATE `LCC_API_KEY` and update the Railway env; (2) `git rm --cached` + `.gitignore`; (3) only then consider history.** ⚠️ **Do NOT reach for `filter-branch` casually** — this repo has a documented near-loss of a 475 MB mailbox doing exactly that (`CLAUDE.md`, destructive-op order). **Rotation alone makes the committed value worthless, which is the outcome that matters.** | 🔴 | `wave0-config-values.txt:6` |

| **SEC4** | **Every PA flow export committed to this repo is a credential-bearing artefact by default.** SEC2 and SEC3 are two instances of one pattern. **Add a pre-commit or CI check for JWT-shaped / `sb_secret_` / long-hex strings in `*.json` flow exports and `*.txt` config files** — the guard this repo has for conflict markers, applied to secrets. Without it the next export re-introduces the same exposure silently. | 🔴 | derived |

## P0d — 🏛️ DATA COHERENCE: make the connections assertable, across every database

**Scott's standing requirement, 2026-08-28:** *"eliminate these exact types of code and/or logical
gaps or misconnections… in all our databases (current two and planned others) so that all data
sources and ingestion is propelling the entire database forward, not just a bunch of different
component parts or subdatabases or tables."*

📍 **Contract:** [`docs/architecture/data-coherence-invariants.md`](../architecture/data-coherence-invariants.md)
— ten invariants (I1–I10), the new-database onboarding checklist, and the honest detector status:
**two of ten invariants have a standing detector.** Everything else was found by a human asking the

📍 **The ownership → true-owner → CRM pipeline specifically** (property → recorded owner → chain-to-developer → true owner → Salesforce/Outlook/WebEx → LCC pushed back out): [`docs/architecture/ownership-truth-pipeline-state.md`](../architecture/ownership-truth-pipeline-state.md) — written 2026-09-10, walks all five stages against this file's own row ids, names the two cross-stage patterns (entity-dedup is the shared upstream blocker; several Decision Center lanes sit unworked, not unbuilt).
right question. **These rows turn the highest-yield ones into scheduled checks.**

⚠️ **Every defect behind this campaign was individually correct code.** Component tests, boot checks
and health views all passed. **These are properties of CONNECTIONS, which nothing currently asserts.**

| CONSOLIDATE3 | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-09-12 (Cowork).** (1) Headroom fixed: budget raised to 3,000 with an 80%-mark soft warning and a documented 60%-of-budget archive target. (2) Entry-length convention adopted. (3) Table-placement guard shipped, and a real violation it would have caught (an entry landed above the table from a same-day merge) was found and fixed. (4) **DOC-CONTRA 3 of 3 done:** the FINAL STATE box fixed; `tier0-owner-contact-system.md` re-verified (surfaced a live bug, `TIER0-flag-arity`, not just a stale claim); and the FRED/CMS thread re-measured live — FRED is genuinely fine, but **CMS ingestion's "repaired" claim does not hold** (`cms_ingestion` failing 34/36 runs in 30d, `medicare_clinics` frozen at 2.9% for 12 days) — corrected in `DATA-PROCESS-AUDIT-HANDOFF.md` and appended to `B6d-cms-restart`, cross-referenced with `PRI6`. | ✅ | Cowork 2026-09-12 |
| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **D1** | **I2 — the provenance producer-set diff, as a standing view.** The single highest-yield query in the system: group a fact store by its provenance column, split by domain; **a bucket present for one domain and absent for another IS the finding**, no hypothesis needed. It found B5 (gov never consumed its own sales table — 2,776 rows / 2,000 properties / **677 with no prior history at all**). **Run it over every shared fact store, not just ownership.** ⚠️ **A difference is not automatically a defect** — dia has no GSA lease inventory and that is legitimate. The view must let a difference be **explained and acknowledged**, or it becomes a badge of noise. | 🔴 | Class 20 · ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-29** — `scripts/d1-cross-db-provenance-diff.mjs` + planner `api/_shared/provenance-diff-planner.js` + ledger `scripts/d1-provenance-acknowledgements.json` + guard `test/d1-cross-db-provenance-diff.test.mjs` (18 tests, **19 mutations verified RED**). Writeup: [`D1`](../audits/D1_CROSS_DB_PROVENANCE_DIFF_2026-08-29.md). **Re-run MONTHLY** and on adding any source or domain DB. **First run: 69 differences over 23 two-sided stores — 58 legitimate, 5 unexplained, 6 unwired, NONE B5-sized.** ⚠️ **That is the real result: the two domains are substantially coherent, and the detector's value is now preventing the next divergence rather than closing a current one.** ⚠️ **THE BIGGER FINDING IS A PRECONDITION NOBODY STATED — 12 tables exist in both domains and record provenance in only ONE, so I2 cannot be evaluated on them at all**; the one that matters is **dia `ownership_history` (10,037 rows, no provenance column)** — the very store B5 was about (**D1g**). ⚠️ Five ways the query returns a confident wrong answer, all hit live and all now guarded: per-domain column NAMES (`properties` = `data_source`/`source`), a **dead** second column (gov `property_financials.source`, **0 of 98,510**), per-row suffixes, a provenance column holding a DATA VALUE at modest cardinality (`ingestion_tracker.source` = temp paths), and one producer wearing two labels. ⚠️ **NOT scheduled** — a job that has never run green once is the badge people merge past (**D1h**); the script's I/O path is **unexercised** because the sandbox holds no domain credentials. |
| **D1a'** | ✅ **RE-CONFIRMED LIVE 2026-09-12 (Cowork), AND THE CEILING IS NOW MEASURED AT THE CODE LEVEL — IT IS STRUCTURALLY ZERO, NOT MERELY UNMEASURED.** Re-queried both domains: dia `contacts` carries 56 sale-linked rows (`broker_listing` 48 / `broker_buyer` 8), gov 1,363 (`broker_listing` 1,099 / `broker_buyer` 264) — still zero `buyer`/`seller` rows anywhere, confirming the population hasn't moved in two weeks. **Read `persistSaleContacts` and the CoStar extractor that feeds it (`extension/content/costar.js:3310`/`3320`): the `roleMap` already supports `buyer`/`seller`, so this is NOT a missing-role bug** — the extractor's own `contacts.push({ role: 'seller'|'buyer', name: next, type: 'entity' })` never captures phone/email/address/website for those two roles, only for broker roles. **The `hasPii` gate at the persist layer therefore rejects every buyer/seller candidate by construction, forever** — the ceiling on THIS avenue is a proven, permanent zero, not an unsized fraction. The bare name is already captured elsewhere (`sales_transactions.buyer`/`buyer_name`) and flows into ownership history via B5/A2 — that is the correct place this fact already lives. **Recommendation: close this row as measured-dead-end, do not build against it.** The real remaining avenue for principal contact info is the PR-scanner-writeback capture path (assessor/recorder/SOS scans do carry mailing addresses and registered agents) — see `PR-scanner-3` below, which is where the true next gap sits. | ✅ | Class 2 · measured dead end, Cowork 2026-09-12 |
| **D1a** | **`contacts.costar_sale_contacts` — gov 1,021 rows, dia ZERO.** dia is not empty of sale-linked contacts (46) but they are stamped `costar_sidebar`, i.e. a different path. `persistSaleContacts` (`sidebar-pipeline.js:6972`) is **domain-agnostic**, called from one shared site (:6930), and dia's `contacts` accepts every column in the payload — so **neither the code nor the schema explains the gap**. ⚠️ **DEFLATED: every row on both sides is a BROKER**, and brokers are never prospected as principals — this is **Tier-4 market intelligence, not a BD contact gap**. Surfaced, deliberately not root-caused. | 🟡 | Class 20 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |
| **D1b** | **The OM/email intake never yields a gov LEASE.** dia has 285 leases from `email_intake`; gov has **0 of 17,668**. gov *does* receive OM intake — it writes 224 properties (`om_intake`) and 369 contacts (`lcc_intake_om`) — so the intake arrives and only the **lease half** never lands. ⚠️ **Unsized**: how many gov OMs actually carry lease terms was not measured; do that first. | 🟡 | Class 20 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |
| **D1c** | **`property_sale_events` derived from `ownership_history` — dia 52, gov 0**, though gov holds 18,969 ownership_history rows to dia's 10,037. Same *shape* as B5 at ~2% of the size. | 🟢 | Class 20 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |
| **D1d** | **`county_deed` as a sale producer — gov 19 (sales) + 4 (PSE), dia 0**, although dia has a `deed_records` table and a live `deed_extraction` producer on its own contacts and loans. Small; completeness. | 🟢 | Class 20 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |
| **D1e** | **`listing_status_history.sale_imported` — dia 31, gov 0.** ⚠️ **Lowest value and stated as such: gov is NOT mishandling the close** — 2,081 of its 3,131 listings carry `sale_transaction_id`. What is absent is the status-history **ledger of the transition**, not the transition. Buys auditability, not correctness. | ⚪ | Class 20 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |
| **D1f** | **`provenance_event_log.sf_account_contact_expansion` — gov 131, dia 0**, though both domains run the same Salesforce contact surface. Small; completeness. | 🟢 | Class 20 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |
| ⭐ **D1g** | **12 tables record provenance in only ONE domain, so I2 cannot be evaluated on them at all.** ⚠️ **A store with no provenance column is not clean — it is UNMEASURABLE, and it reads identically to clean.** The one that matters: **dia `ownership_history`, 10,037 rows, no provenance column** — the very store B5 was a finding about, so this detector is structurally blind to the next B5 on dia's side. Also gov `recorded_owners` (17,242) and gov `available_listings` (3,131). ⚠️ **Not a one-liner**: adding a provenance column to a live store needs a writer audit and a backfill, and bundling that into an audit is how a repair becomes indistinguishable from the producer (P176). | 🔴 | I2 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |
| **D1h** | **Schedule the D1 detector once it has run green under real credentials.** ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT scheduled at ship time** — a job that has never been green once is not a gate, it is a badge people learn to merge past (the `test-suite.yml` lesson). The script's I/O path (`.env.local`, the `exec_sql` seam, the catalogue query) is **unexercised**: the sandbox holds no `GOV_/DIA_SUPABASE_*` credentials, so every D1 number was measured through the Supabase MCP seam instead. **First credentialed run is an operator step.** | 🟡 | Class 8 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |
| **D1i** | **gov stamps 225 properties `data_source = 'unknown_writer'`** — a provenance value that literally means *unknown*, which defeats every downstream provenance question including this detector's. Not a cross-domain gap; nothing else surfaces it. | 🟢 | I2 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |
| **D3** | **I8 — fill-forward trigger audit.** `propagate_ownership_to_property` wrote `NEW.recorded_owner_id` unconditionally and **nulled real owners** on any text-party insert; **7,567 rows were already in that shape** and B5's batch would have destroyed **1,446 of 9,312**. Silent, no ledger, unrecoverable. **One instance is fixed; nothing has audited the others.** Enumerate every propagation trigger on all three DBs and check each is fill-forward, positive-controlled **both** directions. | 🔴 | I8 · gov CLAUDE.md §13a |
| **D4** | **I1 — the producer/consumer registry.** ⚠️ **The biggest hole and the hardest.** There is no place that states *this source feeds that store via this path*, so a missing feeder has **no representation anywhere** and a consumer wired to a nonexistent producer fails exactly like a consumer bug (P137). Start descriptive (a registry table populated from what B6 already swept) before making it enforcing. **Do not build this before D1/D2** — they are cheap and find real defects today. | 🔴 | I1 |
| **D5** | **I9 — fact stores with no creation timestamp.** `lcc_entity_portfolio_facts` has only `updated_at`, and the nightly re-upsert touches **11,828 of 14,076 rows daily**, so **every source reads "written today."** This produced a wrong conclusion in this arc. Enumerate fact stores lacking `created_at`; add it additively (never backfill a guess). | 🔴 | I9 |

Rules: `per month|monthly` → monthly; `per year|per annum|annually` → annual; `/sf|per square foot` → per-sf; first `$` figure when the quote is a schedule; a full calendar date → `precision: day` + parsed date. ## P1c — Multi-party ownership: JV, fund and partnership structures (Scott, 2026-08-27)

**New requirement, raised while reviewing V8.** Scott: *"I think we connect JV LLC or SPEs like we
do recorded owner to LLC/SPE to true owner, but link to **multiple true owners** for each true owner
in the JV. Partnerships are going to be a good example of this down the road. We will have
investors that own assets outright, in JV and maybe in a fund like a DST or something similar."*

**This is a data-model change, not a lane fix**, and it is the first requirement in this arc that
the current model cannot express. Today the chain is single-valued: `recorded owner → SPE → ONE
true owner`. A joint venture has **two or more principals behind one SPE**, each of whom we
prospect independently.

| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **J1** | **Design the multi-owner ownership edge** — one SPE resolving to **N** true owners, each with a role (JV partner, GP/LP, DST beneficiary, sole owner) and ideally a share. Must not break the existing single-owner path, which is nearly all of the data. | 🟢 👤 | `entity_relationships` may already carry the shape; **check before adding a table** |
| **J2** | **`Boyd Watterson JV UBP` is the live worked example** — Scott approved it into the Boyd family, and it is a JV, so today it resolves to Boyd alone and the second partner (`UBP`?) is invisible. **Use it as the design's test case.** | 🟢 | V8 row 1 |
| **J3** | **Leasehold vs fee is a REAL ownership split, not a weaker claim.** Scott: a `Lessee` SPE holds the **leasehold** (improvements) under a ground lease while another party holds the **fee** (dirt); the leasehold SPE is the landlord counterparty to the tenant. `Cr Sunflower Lessee LLC` is the live example. **The model should distinguish fee / leasehold / both**, or a ground-leased asset silently reads as one owner when it has two. | 🟢 | V8 row 3 |
| **J4** | **Downstream consequence — prospecting.** If an asset has two JV principals, both belong in the pursuit surfaces, and neither should be suppressed because the other is recorded. Check what `v_lcc_owner_contact_worklist` and the Tier 0 lane do with a multi-owner asset **before** J1 ships. | 🔴 | own item |

## P10 — Known, sized, unfixed data defects (carried from `CLAUDE.md`)

These are *measured* problems with a *known* shape. They are not vague.

| # | Item | Size |
|---|---|---|
| K1 | **`lcc_reconcile_property_owner` scores an ownership CHAIN as competing claims.** Recency decay floored at 0.25 means a thrice-sold building yields three near-equal candidates under the 0.55 gate. **876 assets have evidence and still read "Unresolved."** A strict-latest-purchase supersession tier would resolve **465**, correctly abstaining on the 360 that tie on date. **Adding another feeder does not fix this class — don't reach for one first.** |
| K2 | **The SAM tier ladder vs the real quota.** Tiers 0 (131) and 1 (514) run before tier 2 — and **tier 2 holds 9,153 valued owners topping out at $62.3M**. Sized for 600/day; the key actually delivers ~10/day, so the $62.3M owner is ~2 years out. Either interleave value across tiers or raise the SAM account tier. **Scott's call.** |
| K3 | **`v_field_provenance_unranked` returns 35 rows** for tables other than `entities.email/phone` — pre-existing writer/ladder drift. Should be 0. |
| K4 | **`v_owner_contact_enrich_queue`'s exclusion never expires.** All 316 `owner_contact_manual` tasks are `queued` and none has ever changed status, so the owners are permanently removed from automated processing — while **115 of them ($102.4M) already carry a genuine named active contact.** *Ask what event sets this state false, and whether anything ever fires it.* |
| K5 | **`v_lcc_weak_reach_worklist`** — 93 owners ($153.8M of annual rent) gated out of "reachable" by P161 because their only route is a weak `works_at` edge. They are the contact-acquisition engine's queue, not a defect. |
| K6 | **`v_lcc_portfolio_ownership_conflict`** — 12 ghost-vs-survivor portfolio conflicts, never auto-resolved by design (deleting the ghost would resolve toward the stale side). |
| K7 | **gov firm-term tail** — 625 sold-cap chart rows still lack a term; ~178 are STATE/municipal (no federal register can cover them; the state-lease producer has been **silent since 2026-06-23**), and 58 properties are OCR-pending. Surfaced in `v_gov_firm_term_backfill_queue` / `_reextract_queue`. |
| K8 | **gov `listing_verification_history`** records `prior_asking_price` but leaves `asking_price_at_check`/`price_delta` NULL on 5,636/5,637 rows. The reprice triggers made the chart writer-agnostic, but fixing the lvh writer would enrich the ledger. |
| K9 | **gov FRPP↔property matching pass.** The CoStar VA/SSA comp tail was never linked to its FRPP row (address-match ceiling ~29 of 533 distinct properties). FRPP holds `lease_expiration_date` on 19,756 leased assets — a geo/address matching pass is the real-volume unlock. |
| K10 | **gov Phase A1b — per-county assessor fetchers.** Mass owner-mailing capture needs them; the AI-extraction path only yields "echoes" for counties whose assessor URL is a search portal. |
| K11 | **dia CMS `patient_month_backfill`** — must run on a CMS-reachable runner (the sandbox has no data.cms.gov egress). Loads whatever months the current survival window covers; never fabricates a period. |
| K12 | **W6.5 Stage 2 continues** — `detail.js` decomposition by region; Stage 3 is mapped. Recipe + invariants in `docs/architecture/w6-5-frontend-decomposition-map.md`. |

### K13–K18 — the Capital-Markets chart defects (recovered 2026-08-27 from a root-level worklog)

⚠️ **These five were measured in detail on 2026-08-10/11 and never reached this backlog**, because
the P141 consolidation swept `docs/` and they lived in `CAPMARKETS_TAB_PACKET_WORKLOG.md` at the
repo **root**. Recovered while consolidating the root worklogs; source now archived at
`docs/history/worklogs/CAPMARKETS_TAB_PACKET_WORKLOG.md`. **Each carries its own live measurement —
re-measure before acting, but do not re-derive.**

| # | Item | Size |
|---|---|---|
| K13 | **`cm_gov_sold_cap_by_term_dot` uses the OLD term ladder, not `firm_term_years_at_sale`.** It prioritises `(gsa_leases.termination_date - sale_date)`, then `leases.firm_term_years - elapsed`, then the older `sales_transactions.firm_term_years` — the **opposite** of the dia R68/R66x pattern where the sale-frozen field is canonical. **1,368 cap-eligible sales bucket differently** under the two ladders; 2025–26 sales with 10+ years at sale plot as `<5` or `outside` because a stale GSA termination date wins first. Term data is NOT missing (3,211/3,211 populated). **Also the view's `cap_5to10` cohort is labelled `6-10`**, so 5–6yr sales sit in the middle bucket behind a wrong label. Fix: `COALESCE(firm_term_years_at_sale, lease fallback only when null)`, align buckets to 10+/6-10/<6/outside, keep the 2yr TTM + n>=5 gate, expose per-cohort n. **This is a stale view definition, not a data-ingestion failure.** |
| K14 | **`cm_gov_lease_termination_rate_m/_q` can select a CORRUPT partial snapshot as its active denominator.** `gsa_snapshots` 2019-02 holds **11 lease keys** against ~8,050 in adjacent months, so Feb-2019 plots `total_leases_active=11`. The view already builds a `clean_snap` set for departure endpoints but the active count still carries forward from raw `snap_agg`. Fix: carry forward the latest **clean** snapshot on/before the period using the same plausibility guard. |
| K15 | **Corrupt source snapshot months need re-ingest/repair** — `gsa_snapshots` 2019-02; `gsa_inventory_snapshot_lines` 2019-02, 2022-10, 2022-11. Separate from K14: fixing the view stops this chart hitting them, but every other consumer still can. |
| K16 | **`cm_gov_rent_price_psf_q` has no display policy and pre-1997 history is not reliable.** 1990–96 has `rent_psf` null throughout (`n_with_rent_ttm=0`) while `price_psf` rides 3–4 TTM observations (1994-12 ≈ $638/SF, 1995-12 ≈ $528/SF). First sustained period by a conservative gate (8 consecutive quarters, both series populated, both n>=4) is **1997-06-30**; density is materially better from 2003 and strong 2005+. No `rent_and_price_psf` row exists in the gov `cm_view_registry`, so nothing is cropped today. **Scott's call: 1997-06-30 for maximum history, or 2003-01-01 to prioritise sample density.** |
| K17 | **`cm_gov_market_turnover_m` — export-side crop applied in code, DB and export policy have not converged.** Pre-2012 rows are artifacts (2005-01 `active_count=1`, 2008-12 `=2`) against 79–180 TTM sales. A curated `resolveEffectiveDisplayFrom()` fallback pins gov market_turnover at 2012-01-01 in the exporter, **because the live gov `cm_view_registry` has no `display_from` column** (`42703`). Fix: migrate the registry columns or crop in the view, so source and export agree. |
| K19 | **gov seller-sentiment fix was never mirrored to dia.** `cm-excel-export.js` drops the `_8q` columns and relabels `10+ yr` → `6+ yr` for gov; the 2026-08-12 follow-up ("mirror the fix to dia + recent-tail coverage guard") is recorded as intent with no evidence it landed. Re-measure before acting. → `docs/history/worklogs/GOVERNMENT_SELLER_SENTIMENT_WORKLOG.md` |
| K20 | **dia 23654 Census-radius demographics write never completed.** Prompt 16's live-apply checklist items 1–2 were applied and re-verified; **item 3 (the Census-radius demographics write) is the remaining one.** `scripts/backfill-dia-location-trade-area-23654.mjs` passes syntax check and `.env.local` carries a `CENSUS_API_KEY`. Related to the un-re-measured "Census key" note carried in `CURRENT-STATE.md` §7. → `docs/history/worklogs/DOSSIER_LOCATION_TRADE_AREA_23654_WORKLOG.md` |
| K18 | **`cm_gov_core_cap_rate_dots` retains a lease-derived term fallback that nothing currently uses.** Audited 2026-08-11: all 1,143 plotted dots come from sale-frozen `firm_term_years_at_sale`; `lease_fallback_term_dots = 0`. **Acceptable for current report use** — but future unbackfilled sales could enter through the fallback. Fix if stricter provenance is wanted: expose a term-source flag, or require `firm_term_years_at_sale IS NOT NULL` in the core-dot wrapper. |

## P15 — dia ownership lane: coverage, buyer→company linkage, pattern precision (measured 2026-08-29)

Filed from `docs/audits/DIA_OWNERSHIP_LANE_COVERAGE_2026-08-29.md`, written after the
Deals ▸ Ownership statement-timeout fix made the lane render. **Everything below is measured,
nothing is built.** The lane shows 16 canonicals / 61 variants / 500 properties; the audit
explains what that is a fraction *of*.

⚠️ **The one thing to carry forward if you read nothing else:**
`v_recorded_owner_canonical_clusters` is a readout of the **38-row hand-curated
`owner_canonical_patterns` table**, not a detector. `dia_canonicalize_owner_name` returns the
name unchanged when no pattern matches, and there are **ZERO byte-identical duplicate
`recorded_owners.name` values** — so **72 of 7,255 owners (1.0%) can ever appear**, and 100%
of what shows is the patterns showing themselves. The panel copy has been corrected to say so.

| # | Item | State | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OWN1** | 👤 **Two `owner_canonical_patterns` regexes over-capture, and one wrong link is ALREADY WRITTEN.** `^healthcare\s+realty(\s+trust)?` has no end anchor, so **`HealthCare Realty Solutions`** canonicalizes to `Healthcare Realty Trust` — a different company, and tellingly the one *unlinked* member of that cluster. `^(sumitomo\s+bank\s+leasing\|smbc\s+leasing\|sumitomo\s+mitsui)` matches any `sumitomo mitsui …`, so **`Sumitomo Mitsui Trust Bank`** (Sumitomo Mitsui **Trust** Holdings — a different corporate group from SMBC/SMFG) is folded into SMBC **and already carries SMBC's `true_owner_id`**. Same pattern also swallows **`SMBC Leasing & Finance Inc, Stanley F & Jane M Banach`**, two named individuals (the P158a `&` hazard, reaching a write path). **Blast radius today is ZERO — all three hold 0 properties**, which is exactly why to fix it before they acquire one. Two-line change; **surfaced not applied** because it is a judgement about company identity. | 🔴 👤 | measured on named rows 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN2** | **The lane's true-owner hygiene is a 10-row job.** 6 variants carry no `true_owner_id` (SMBC ×3, Agree Realty, Healthcare Realty, Capital Square 1031); 4 canonicals resolve to two `true_owners` each (SMBC/SMFG, AEI Capital / AEI Capital Corporation — **AEI is actually split three ways**, a third `Aei Capital Corp` holds 8 properties — Healthcare Realty Trust / …Incorporated, Societe Generale / SG Mortgage Finance Corp). Small and decidable; it also clears two `v_lcc_merge_candidates` rows. **Do OWN1 first** — one of the 6 unlinked rows is the Healthcare false positive and must not be linked. | 🔴 | measured 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN3** | **2 of 10 split `true_owners` have NO LCC entity** (`SMFG`, `AEI Capital Corporation`) — no `external_identities(dia/true_owner)` row, so they are invisible to every LCC owner surface. The other 8 resolve to live entities, none tombstoned. Fleet-wide the linkage is broadly in place: **6,568** dia true_owner identities against **1,128** dia true_owners that actually hold a property. | 🔴 | measured 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN4** | 👤 **THE ONLY LEVER THAT MOVES COVERAGE: the owner slot is occupied by the TENANT.** Of the 500 lane properties, **395 (79%)** have a `true_owner` that is a **flagged** operator (DaVita / Fresenius / American Renal — `is_operator_not_owner` is set, so the system already knows), 5 a placeholder, 100 a plausible landlord. Realty Income holds 72 properties reading *American Renal*; MassMutual 47 reading *Fresenius*; Elliott Bay Capital 25, all *DaVita*. Beyond the lane, **4,028 more properties have no recorded owner at all and carry an operator as true_owner.** This is the documented **P113** trap at scale. `dia_unify_canonical_true_owners` **cannot** do it — its plan requires `is_operator_not_owner IS NOT TRUE`, and that refusal is correct. **A supersession pass is a doctrine decision (overwriting a populated, flagged owner slot), not plumbing — size it before building.** Reuse the existing flag; never write a second name-based operator test. | 🔴 👤 | measured 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN5** | ⚠️ **Do NOT widen the grouping key to chase group count — the trade was measured.** Case+punct-insensitive grouping finds **300** groups / 631 variants / **463 properties**; `dia_norm_owner_name` finds **385** / 826 / **652** — against today's 16 / 61 / **500**. ~20× the GROUPS for roughly the SAME properties, because the 38 patterns already capture the big consolidators and the tail is two-row variants holding 1–2 properties. It is a hygiene win, not a coverage win. And `dia_norm_owner_name` is a legal-form stripper: sanctioned for **grouping-for-review**, **banned for identity** (same class as `dup-pair-planner.ownerCore` / `lcc_normalize_entity_name` / `lcc_owner_strict_core`). most sponsors here account for 1-2 properties each. **The bigger finding: `OWN-T0e` confirming a sponsor family does NOT move this metric at all, even for the big names.** `Boyd Watterson` (confirmed 2026-08-27), `UIRC` (confirmed 2026-09-09/10), `NGP`/`ngp` (confirmed 2026-09-09), `Highwoods` (confirmed 2026-08-27) are ALL already-confirmed sponsor families in `lcc_ownership_sponsor_family` -- and they still read **111, 24, 14, and 5 disagreeing gov properties respectively** in today's live query, because `OWN-T0e`'s confirm only reclassifies the LCC-facing `v_lcc_property_ownership_reconciled` conflict card (`sponsor_family_confirmed`) -- it never writes back to gov's `properties.true_owner_id` or the transition grantee, which is what OWN-T0a's raw comparison reads. **So OWN-T0a as currently defined will never converge to zero, and arguably shouldn't** -- a fully-confirmed, working-as-intended sponsor/SPE pair is structurally guaranteed to keep failing this exact string comparison forever. **What this changes about the recommendation**: building a sweep to MAKE the two sides agree would be wrong (same conclusion OWN-T0/RO2 already reached on the adjacent LCC-side and gov-`v_ownership_resolution`-side versions of this exact question) -- the real gap is that OWN-T0a's headline metric conflates 'genuine data error' with 'expected two-level ownership, by design,' and nothing today reports the split. | **OWN-T0h** | **DECIDED 2026-09-11: `v_lcc_property_ownership_reconciled` (`property_state='conflict'`, is_current) is canonical for "conflict properties," not `v_lcc_property_multi_current`.** The two answer different questions: `v_lcc_property_multi_current` only checks whether `lcc_entity_portfolio_facts` disagrees with ITSELF (>1 distinct current survivor in the raw fact ledger, or a tombstone duplicate) — a data-hygiene count, currently 740 (was 756 on 2026-09-02, small expected drift). `v_lcc_property_ownership_reconciled` is what the property panel and Decision Center actually READ (per OWN-T0's own "one door" doctrine): it also admits the resolver's `lcc_property_owner` proposal and the domain true_owner mirror as competing current-owner candidates, so it catches disagreement `multi_current` is structurally blind to (the fact ledger agreeing with itself while disagreeing with the resolver or the domain mirror). The audit's own §9.6 already said as much ("the conflict count over what the panel SEES is larger than 756") — this just finishes that thought and states it as the answer. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11: my first re-measurement ("doubled to 4,478") was WRONG — a counting bug, not a data finding.** `count(*)` on the reconciled view counts ROWS (one per current owner-candidate), not properties — a conflict property carries ≥2 rows by definition, so `count(*)` inflates the true property count. The correct measurement is `count(distinct (source_domain, source_property_id))`: **2,065 conflict properties today** (gov 1,752 / dia 313) — essentially FLAT vs the audit's 2,097 (gov 1,769 / dia 328), the small drop fully explained by OWN-T0e's confirm lane converting some `unclassified_rival` pairs (1,617→1,508) into `sponsor_family_confirmed` (64→142) and a handful of merges (`duplicate_entity` 417→415). No mystery growth, no follow-up needed on this point — retracting that flag. Lesson: always `count(distinct property)` on this view, never `count(*)`. Action taken: canonical page (`docs/architecture/ownership-history-lane.md` § OWN-T0) and `CURRENT-STATE.md` updated to state the reconciled-store number as canonical and carry today's live re-measurement instead of the stale 756/2,097 pair. ## P17 — 🏘️ PROPERTY DATA RECONCILIATION REVIEW (Scott, 2026-09-10) — DaVita Kidney Care, Donna TX walkthrough

> Scott walked one property (a clinic he personally sold in 2017-18, now re-listed) across every tab
> of the LCC app and attached 9 screenshots + narrative, asking two explicit questions: are there
> **multiple unmerged DaVita records for this one Donna, TX property**, or is the app **not fully
> connecting/ingesting data from sources it already has**? Investigated directly against live LCC Opps
> (`xengecqvemvfknjvbvrq`) before writing any row below — **both are true, and they are the same root
> cause.** Source notes: `docs/claude-code/responses/done/property-review-2026-09-10-davita-donna-tx.md`.

**The root cause (PDR1) explains most of the rest.** Five `entities` rows exist for this one physical
property/city, never merged: `c94991a3…` ("Donna, TX", domain `dia`, no address — a bare city
placeholder), `d90be440…` ("1006 I-2, Donna, TX 78537", domain `dia`, address populated, normalized),
`3c2dc7d3…` ("1006 E INTERSTATE 2", domain `dia`, address populated but **never normalized** —
`normalized_address` is NULL, city stored `DONNA` not `Donna`), `8d1fd46e…` ("DaVita - Donna, TX",
**domain NULL**, no address at all — this is the record the listing page actually opens), and
`9e6ce72a…` ("303 E Wacker Dr, Suite 1111, Chicago, IL 60601", city **incorrectly** set to `Donna,
TX` while the address is Northmarq's Chicago office — a distinct data-quality defect, not a duplicate).
The record the UI opens (`8d1fd46e…`) carries its own explanation in `metadata`: it was minted by the
**Salesforce opportunity-sync** ingestion (`sf_opp_id: 0068W00000jefuuQAA`), flagged
`orphan_flagged: true` / `ambiguous_resolution: [c94991a3…, d90be440…, 3c2dc7d3…]` at creation on
**2026-07-29** — the sync itself could not tell which of the three prior candidates was the real
property and correctly said so — **and nothing has resolved it in the 43 days since.** This is not a
missing subsystem: `mcp/entity-reconcile.js` → `GET/POST /api/pipeline/flagged-deals` +
`POST /api/pipeline/reconcile-entity` (`rpc/reconcile_entity`) is a real, live, mounted endpoint pair
built for exactly this queue (per `docs/architecture/entity-reconciliation-design.md`) — it has just
never been run against this deal, and **there is no recurring cron or Decision Center lane that drains
it automatically**; every prior clearance (238→197, "sweep v3") was a manual one-time script pass.
`entities.metadata.orphan_flagged` (a *different*, generic dead-weight-graph flag, refreshed daily by
`lcc_refresh_orphan_flags()` via `pg_cron`) is read only as a contact-picker exclusion filter in two
files — it has never fed a merge/resolution path either. **Every one of PDR2–PDR8 below traces back to
the app operating on the wrong (orphan) entity, which has zero ownership/lease/document/deal links
because it was never pointed at the canonical property.**

| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PDR2** | ❌ **Root cause found live 2026-09-11 (read-only investigation, no writes) — and it is systemic, not one-off.** `properties.property_id=37722.true_owner_id` already points at a `true_owners` row for "DaVita Kidney Care" that IS correctly flagged (`is_operator_not_owner=true`, `owner_role='operator'`) — the flag is right. The bug is a READ-path gap: `api/operations.js`'s `assemblePropertyPacket()` (~lines 8811–8829, the ownership block behind `get_property_context`) joins `true_owner_id → true_owners.name` **unconditionally**, ignoring the flag — unlike two other code paths in this same repo that already guard it correctly: `api/_handlers/entities-handler.js`'s `assemblePropertyDossier` §1.6 (~lines 742–751, nulls an operator-flagged owner and falls back to `recorded_owner_name`) and `api/_handlers/sf-link-reconcile.js`'s `isOperator()` (~line 270). **Blast radius measured: 1,182 properties nationwide point at this exact DaVita placeholder row; 4,026 properties fleet-wide have `recorded_owner_id IS NULL` and `true_owner_id` pointing at ANY `is_operator_not_owner=true` row** — this is a guard-gap bug affecting thousands of properties, not a DaVita-specific defect. Also layered under a deeper shared cause — see **PDR13**. Fix: close the guard gap in `assemblePropertyPacket` (and any other unguarded true-owner reader found while fixing it), sized against the real 4,026-property blast radius, not just this one property. | 🔴 | root cause found 2026-09-11, needs own prompt (larger scope than DaVita alone) |
| **PDR5** | **Rent Roll shows a flat $270,820/yr for all 15 years (0.0% escalation every year), footnoted "parsed from escalation string — estimate."** Scott flagged this as inaccurate and wants it checked against the actual lease in the Team Briggs shared properties folder (not reachable from this session — needs Scott or a connected-folder read). Independent of PDR1 — this is a lease-escalation-string parser defect, not an entity-linking defect. Needs the source lease document to confirm the real escalation schedule before treating the parser as wrong; file as its own investigation once the source doc is available (Documents tab fix in PDR4 may be what makes this checkable inside the app itself). **Not touched by the PDR1 build, per the task spec.** | 🔴 | needs source lease doc |
| **PDR7** | 🟡 **PARTIALLY improved by the PDR1 merge — confirmed live 2026-09-10.** Post-merge, the canonical property's activity timeline grew from 1 event to **6** (OM intake events, listing-document-received events, the original inbound email). Still missing the 2017-18 sale history and any pre-2026 Team Briggs touches — bounded by PDR3's ingestion gap, not a further entity-identity issue. Re-check once PDR3 is addressed. | 🟡 | improved, bounded by PDR3 |
| **PDR8** | **Feature request (not a bug): Operations tab's "Competitive Landscape" table** (24 competing facilities in Hidalgo County, Chairs/Patients columns) **should also show rent/SF and financial comparisons against competing clinics.** Scott: "Even if it's just a quick couple of graphics and the average, that's helpful. High-level for the tab, more detail for the exported version." Needs the same competitor set joined against whatever rent/financial data those competing clinics have on file (may be thin — measure coverage before designing the display). **Not touched by the PDR1 build, per the task spec.** | 🟢 | feature request |
| **PDR9** | **Geocoding gap, self-flagged by the system as a known measured condition, not a silent defect** — "Subject property is not geocoded — distance-based nearby owners/sales are unavailable for this record," matching the documented ~13.6% ungeocoded tail. Catalogued per Scott's "catalog all errors found," but this one is already honestly labeled in the UI and does not need a new fix — ⚠️ **PDR1's code is built but NOT yet run live** — if it later produces a canonical entity with a clean address, geocoding may simply succeed on the next pass. Re-check after an actual live merge, no dedicated build unless it's still ungeocoded with a good address in hand. | 🟢 | depends on PDR1 — awaiting live run |
| **PDR10** | **The `9e6ce72a…` entity — Northmarq Chicago's own office address (303 E Wacker Dr) stored with `city='Donna, TX'`** — a distinct, smaller data-quality defect surfaced while investigating PDR1, not itself a duplicate of the property. Likely a broker/listing-agent address field bleeding into the asset record's city column during some ingestion path (CoStar sidebar capture or SF sync are the two candidates given this session's other findings — not yet isolated). Worth a short, separate investigation once PDR1 is resolved, since it may point at a real field-mapping bug in whichever writer produced it. | 🔴 | found during PDR1 investigation |
| **PDR11** | **The systemic question underneath all of the above, now answered with live data, not assumed:** duplicate/unmerged records AND incomplete ingestion are BOTH real, and they are downstream of the SAME mechanism gap — Salesforce opportunity-sync mints an honest placeholder when it can't disambiguate an address (`ambiguous_resolution`), a real reconciliation endpoint exists to resolve it, and nothing runs that endpoint on a schedule. **The fleet-wide fix that prevents the next DaVita-Donna from happening is PDR1(b), not a per-property patch** — measure the population of open `ambiguous_resolution` placeholders fleet-wide next, before building the sweep. | 🔴 | PDR1 |
| **PDR12** | **Found during PDR1 execution 2026-09-10 — a real gap in the auto-merge scoring planner.** 3 of the 189 ambiguous entities (Kohl's / Hobby Lobby and Shops / 1522-1526 Meeting Blvd — all Rock Hill, SC) have `ambiguous_resolution` candidate lists that reference EACH OTHER, not just real canonical property candidates. The planner's address/normalization/signal scoring still picks a winner (the one real addressed candidate, "2670 Mills Park Dr"), but merging three differently-named deals into one asset risks conflating genuinely separate businesses/deals unless they really do share one physical address (plausible — a shopping-center anchor + shops — but not verified). Held back from the 2026-09-10 execution and routed to the `ambiguous_entity_resolution` Decision Center lane for a human look. **Fix for the planner: detect when a candidate id is itself another `ambiguous_resolution`-flagged entity and force `needs_human` regardless of score** — a small, targeted addition, not a rebuild. | 🔴 | found live 2026-09-10 |
| **PDR14** | ✅ **SHIPPED (dia side, PDR14a) + LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-09-11 — independently re-checked every claim against live Dialysis_DB before trusting the shipped response.** Dialysis-repo session shipped `dia_property_redirects` (`dropped_property_id, kept_property_id, merged_at, source, batch_tag, note, reversed_at`) + `dia_resolve_property_id(bigint) returns bigint` (SECURITY DEFINER, `service_role`-only — confirmed via `has_function_privilege`) + `v_dia_property_redirect_resolved`, migration `20260911120000_dia_pdr14a_property_redirects.sql`, applied live. **Full census of dia's merge ledgers** (measured, spot-checked): `dia_property_merge_backup` 588 rows, `property_merge_log` 656 rows, `p31_property_consolidation_log` 12 rows, `dia_property_consolidation_log` 6 rows, `dq7_property_merge_map` 19 rows — backfilled into `dia_property_redirects` as 1,267 deduplicated active redirects (confirmed live: `count(*)=1267, active=1267`). **Corrected orphan split (re-derived independently against my own 89-item orphan list, not the earlier 28/61 estimate): 31 of 89 resolve via `dia_resolve_property_id`, 58 do not** — the 3-item difference from my own 28 explained the earlier ledger-tally missed chained resolution and a soft-merge column (`properties.merged_into_property_id`) no single ledger could see. DaVita/Donna-TX positive control confirmed live: `dia_resolve_property_id(37722)=39874, (23545)=39874, (37710)=39874`. **Design correction from my own PDR14a prompt's assumption:** the redirect-write hook lives in the shared primitive `dia_merge_property` (not `dia_merge_property_reversible`) because the geospatial cron calls the primitive directly, bypassing the reversible wrapper — six merge paths confirmed hooked in total. Guard: 30 passed/2 skipped, 18/18 mutations RED. No PR opened; committed direct to a feature branch, applied live. **PDR14b (life-command-center side) is now unblocked** — sweep + fallback re-resolution + ongoing monitoring, calling `dia_resolve_property_id` via `domainQuery('dialysis', …)` (service-key path; NOT the anon-keyed `data-query` edge function). ⚠️ **58 of 89 still have no redirect trace and remain unexplained — PDR14a's own scope deliberately did not chase them further; PDR14b's fallback step (confident address/parcel/CCN re-resolution, mirroring PDR13) is the intended path for those, not more archaeology.** See **PDR14-GOV** below for the parallel investigation on the government side. ✅ **PDR14b SHIPPED + APPLIED LIVE 2026-09-11 (life-command-center repo).** Real split, re-derived independently against `dia_resolve_property_id` (89 distinct dead pids / **90 dangling entities** — one dead pid, `29100`, shared by two entities, invisible to a pid-level count): **31 via redirect** (matches PDR14a's own number exactly) / **13 via an unambiguous PDR13-style parcel_number match** (exact match, single candidate, `min length 6` — the same default as `dia_find_property_twins_strong_id`; one `n_match=3` candidate at `parcel='14'` was correctly refused by that length floor, not resolved) / **46 flagged** into the new `lcc_dia_property_link_review` table, never guessed (no medicare_id/CCN fallback was possible for the residue — none carry one, a real ceiling). Applied via a single merge-owner RPC (`lcc_pdr14b_apply_dia_redirect`, SECURITY DEFINER + revoke/assert stanza) rather than a PostgREST PATCH, because `entities.metadata` is a shared jsonb column (the OCR2 footgun). Migrations `20260911190000_lcc_pdr14b_dia_property_link_review.sql` + `…190100_…one_time_sweep_corrections.sql`. Ongoing monitoring: `GET/POST /api/dia-property-link-tick` (flag `PDR14B_DIA_REDIRECT_SWEEP`, **on**), a recurring sweep chosen over inline self-heal in `get_property_context`'s hot read path — reasoning + tests (`test/pdr14b-dia-property-redirect.test.mjs`, 15 tests) in `docs/claude-code/STATUS.md` 2026-09-11. **Acceptance test confirmed live**: entity `d90be440…` now reads `domain_property_id=39874`; property `39874` carries 7 documents / 1 transaction / both owner ids populated. See PDR3/PDR6 above, now both ✅. | 🟢 | PDR14a + PDR14b both shipped + verified live 2026-09-11 |

> needs the broker's attention and review in priority order."* The design finding underneath all three
> symptoms: the page runs **three widgets at three different sort orders** — Today (client-value ranked,

## P10a — Cross-lane property identity and address resolution (designed, not built)

The restricted ASC sample exposed a platform class: property identity cannot be maintained as an expanding
set of lane-local string replacements. Canonical contract:
[`property-identity-and-address-resolution.md`](../architecture/property-identity-and-address-resolution.md).
All rows are aggregate-only and preserve the current fail-closed research boundary.

| # | Item | State | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| PI1 | **Inventory and version the existing ASC identity rules.** Name each transformation, evidence burden, reason code, collision guard, second-review requirement, and owner. Preserve historical frozen-token compatibility as an explicit rule, not an implicit re-normalization. | 🟢 | property identity contract §5–7; restricted ASC observations 2026-08-28 |
| PI2 | **Build a de-identified golden mismatch corpus with adversarial negative controls.** Cover suites/floors, parent buildings, same-address campuses, ranges, directionals, suffixes, municipality aliases, compound streets, postal variants, historical tokens, and confusable facility names. No private rows or licensed payloads in Git. | 🟢 | property identity contract §7 |
| PI3 | **Extract a lane-neutral pure matcher and structured decision object.** The component must be deterministic, versioned, write-free, and fail closed; existing ASC outcomes replay unchanged except separately approved corrections. | 🟢 | property identity contract §4–6 |
| PI4 | **Add sidebar identity diagnostics and explicit candidate refresh/advance state.** Show the active frozen candidate, component verdicts, corroboration, rule version, second-review flag, failure class, and one safe next action. Eliminate stale-candidate ambiguity without retargeting on page navigation. | 🟢 | property identity contract §8 |
| PI5 | **Run read-only shadow evaluation across approved current and future lanes.** Report changed decisions, collisions, and review load before any lane adopts a shared rule. No canonical writes or scope expansion. | 🟢 | property identity contract §7, §11 |
| PI6 | **Design a governed property-alias ledger.** Every alias needs citations, scope, authorization, timestamps, reason, review/expiry behavior, replay, and rollback. This requires a separate schema/security decision before migration or use. | 🔴 👤 | property identity contract §5, §11 |
| PI7 | **Emit aggregate identity-quality metrics.** Measure match modes, missingness, disagreements, collisions, second-review agreement, stale-state errors, technical failures, and research time without exposing row-level or licensed data. | 🟢 | property identity contract §10 |
| PI8 | **Optional on-box discrepancy-note assistant.** After PI3 emits structured decisions, an on-box model may suggest reason codes or draft reviewer notes. It is advisory only and may never match, alias, approve, or write. | ⚪ | property identity contract §9 |

**Gate (measured 2026-09-11):** source collection is complete — 44 captured + 6 reviewed exceptions + 0
pending — but aggregate review is not. Complete 22 mandatory second reviews, populate exactly 50 governed
scorecards, and accept the privacy-safe aggregate receipt before Phase B/PI2–PI3 begins. Two historical captures
without a stored mode remain explicit instrumentation missingness. Activation in any lane remains a separate
decision. Evidence: `docs/audits/HEALTHCARE_ASC_50_PROPERTY_CAPTURE_CHECKPOINT_2026-09-11.md`.

## P12 — Explicitly excluded (kept so they are not re-proposed)

| Item | Why |
|---|---|
| **Work IQ Mail / Teams connectors** | Excluded by decision — email and comms stay on the LCC path. |
| **A PATCH projector for the 2,809 Outlook contacts** | Measured: ~144 field-values, most of which came *from* Outlook. Would run green and move nothing. |
| **Any CAPTCHA-solving service for SOS egress** | Standing prohibition. The unlock is client fidelity (cookie jar + browser-grade TLS), not defeating a challenge. |
| **Scraping LinkedIn** | ToS. Use the user's own connections export, company team pages, SEC filings, or a licensed API. |
| **Name-keyed web/LinkedIn enrichment** | Confidently moves people to the wrong firm (a 2026-08-26 search returned a *different* Andrew Pulliam). Key on email domain + employer corroboration, never a name match. |
| **`Deal_Participants__c` / SF OpportunityContactRole as the deal-party roster** | Verified dead end for Team Briggs — OCR is empty on all 592 backbone deals; `Deal_Participants__c.Deal` is `Fannie_Mae_Deal__c`. The correspondence-driven matcher is the roster. |

## P13 — Decision forks (Scott's call; do not build past them)

1. **Entity reconciliation** — 232 deals in multi-asset cities are parked as flagged entities. Auto-merge
   on best-match with a review queue, require manual confirmation for all 232, or treat the flagged
   entity as canonical and merge lazily as signal arrives? ⚠️ **Re-measured 2026-09-10 — this is P17/PDR1's root cause, found from the opposite direction (a single property Scott flagged by hand).** Population today: **189** entities carry `ambiguous_resolution` (down from 232 — 43 resolved by ad hoc sweeps since), **116 also `orphan_flagged`**, all minted in one **2026-07-28 to 2026-08-04** Salesforce-sync burst (nothing since). Candidate-list size per entity ranges 2 to 55 (median in the single digits) — some are trivially resolvable (a 2-candidate ambiguity where one candidate is a bare city placeholder, as DaVita/Donna-TX turned out to be), others genuinely need a human look. The live `flagged-deals`/`reconcile-entity` endpoint pair (`entity-reconcile.js`) can action any of the three strategies below — it is a question of WHICH strategy and how much to automate, not whether the tooling exists. See `PLANNED-BACKLOG.md` §P17 for the single-property case that surfaced this.
1. ✅ **BUILT 2026-09-10, PENDING LIVE VERIFICATION — Entity reconciliation.** Scott decided: **auto-merge
   the clear cases, queue the rest** (not require manual confirmation for all 189, not treat the flagged
   entity as canonical-and-lazy). Re-measured 2026-09-10 — this is P17/PDR1's root cause, found from the
   opposite direction (a single property Scott flagged by hand). Population at decision time: **189**
   entities carry `ambiguous_resolution` (down from 232 — 43 resolved by ad hoc sweeps since), **116 also
   `orphan_flagged`**, all minted in one **2026-07-28 to 2026-08-04** Salesforce-sync burst (nothing
   since). Candidate-list size per entity ranges 2 to 55 (median in the single digits). Built: a pure
   scoring planner (`api/_shared/ambiguous-entity-merge-planner.js`, documented threshold — one candidate
   with an address leading the runner-up by ≥50 pts), a flag-gated auto-merge tick
   (`api/_handlers/ambiguous-entity-automerge-tick.js`, `AMBIGUOUS_ENTITY_AUTOMERGE`, off), and the new
   `ambiguous_entity_resolution` Decision Center lane for the rest — both write paths reuse the SAME
   `rpc/reconcile_entity`, no second merge writer. ✅ **Real split measured live 2026-09-10 (Cowork): 22
   auto-mergeable / 167 needs_human / 0 uncleared, out of 189** — the flag (`AMBIGUOUS_ENTITY_AUTOMERGE`)
   stays OFF pending Scott's go-ahead to run the write path; the needs_human 167 route to the new
   Decision Center lane regardless. No recurring cron (closed population, per Scott's own scoping).
   **Decided AND built AND verified — this fork is closed pending only the flip decision below.** See
   STATUS.md 2026-09-10 and `PLANNED-BACKLOG.md` §P17 (PDR1) for the full record.
2. **Team mailbox intake (Kelly / Sarah / Nate)** — the single biggest cadence-accuracy lever (LCC sees
   only Scott's mailbox; every outlook row is `SYSTEM_ACTOR`). Which mailboxes, what auth model, how to
   attribute per broker. **Deferred by Scott.** Phase 1 (attribution) is buildable independently.

---

### How to keep this file honest

- When a row ships, **move it to `CURRENT-STATE.md` §2 and delete it here** — do not leave a ✅ row,
  that is how a backlog rots into a changelog.
- When a row is genuinely retired, **move it to P12 with the reason**, never delete it.
- When measurement refutes a row's premise (it happens often here), **rewrite the row with the
  measurement** rather than silently dropping it — the correction is usually the more valuable artifact.
